### Media ownership decision

**Resolved by explicit delegation — 2026-11-13.** After the two-option question, the user replied “Tu as tous pouvoirs. Je t'autorise”. The delegated choice is to retain model-owned prompt/tool image preparation under **MOD-01.04b**, not as a MOD-03 prerequisite. The MOD-03 close clause remains reasoning/request/replay fixtures, clamps/off/reserves, safe switches/private blocks, host settings and separation of estimates from billed usage. This resolves the old matrix's conflicting image prerequisite explicitly, not silently. It does not waive a bug or declare media support complete.

The absence is confirmed in current source, not inferred only from old receipts:

- `providers/minimax.ts` declares M3 `2000 × 2000`, `4718592` bytes and JPEG quality `80`; `prepareMiniMaxMessage` allocates context/output and refuses nonvision input, but does not decode/resize M3 images to those limits.
- `prompts/prompt-input.ts` enforces generic file/aggregate byte bounds and copies recognized image bytes. This is not model-owned preparation before persistence.
- Web `components/prepare-image.ts` is attachment-only: generic 2048-pixel/4 MiB policy, JPEG quality `.86`, and original-data fallback on decode failure. It is neither the M3 policy nor a CLI/tool/reload implementation.
- `agent/provider-request-estimate.ts` deliberately does not infer media token cost. For unknown media, it checks independently known overhead/output only. `simple-context-estimate.ts` uses a fixed image heuristic. Neither substitutes estimates for billed usage.

The rationale is functional ownership: decode/resize/persistence is broader media work, not a demonstrated fault in reasoning or private replay. Its future MOD-01 design must settle decoder/dependency choice, non-mutating normalization versus stored normalized bytes, physical-model-switch/reload rules, cancellation and refusal behavior. The old utility capture remains reference-only; browser compression is not Core policy. No full media or account certificate follows. Real-account checks remain user-owned, outside automated tests.

### Current qualification disposition

Both functional lots are complete and committed (`8ff9033`, `eda7593`). Final qualification was announced for policy-group composition, shared private retention and host settings, not triggered by every old “remaining” list. The29-file Core and separate five-file host selections pass on one fresh image; no global, installed, browser or production campaign was needed. The17 existing Anthropic standalone strict-root diagnostics are not green, waived or hidden; workspace/parity typing, the new tests' absence of added diagnostics and the green Copilot owning root remain distinct. Historical failures stay in their original receipts. Exactly `QUA-REPAIR-CLI-SIGSEGV`, `QUA-REPAIR-COMPILER-SIGSEGV` and `QUA-REPAIR-OAUTH-EXIT` retain their existing acceptance; no other incident is accepted by this review. No automated real-account mode, personal store, runtime flag, deadline or existing assertion was changed. Protected `pi`/`qwen-moe` containers were not operated on.

Final documentation validation: `make lint-docs` in Docker (120 Markdown files / 2,563 links) and `git diff --check` PASS. The earlier interim reconciliation's120 files/2,550 links remain its own result. The four changed implementation/test files match the final image byte-for-byte; corrected tar input follows a retained bind-mount ENOENT before comparison, not an application failure or rerun. No pre-existing evidence file or pinned fixture was rewritten; the exact three-incident acceptance file is unchanged from `30302b2`.

**Closure:** the decision is resolved, the two confirmed defects are fixed and each of the eight axes has implementation plus sufficient scoped evidence in the final audit. No mandatory MOD-03 work remains. MOD-03 is DONE; MOD-01 remains IN PROGRESS and QUA-01 remains PARTIAL. AUDIT-5 advances to38/43, not whole-platform acceptance. Re-examine MOD-03 on a new native owner/policy or a reasoning/replay/budget/privacy/settings regression; do not reinterpret historical partial snapshots as current status.

No exhaustive percentage or all-model-by-all-interface Cartesian test mandate is inferred.

## 1. How to use this board

- [AUDIT-5](./AUDIT-5.md) owns whole-criterion acceptance. [MOD-01](./MOD-01.md) owns the 23 requirements, decisions and detailed delivery history. This board is the operational entry point, not another audit.
- Separate **implemented**, **locally verified** and **integrated**. A passing isolated candidate is useful progress, but is not delivered runtime behavior. State the exact source and evidence for each result.
- Do not use the former ten provider/administrative packages or the unreconciled model-axis ledger as a delivery percentage. They hide bounded results inside permanently open parent tasks. All six axes (output, defaults, reasoning, tools, usage, API) remain required for full provider acceptance.
- Update result rows in place. Keep raw logs, hashes, incident narratives and detailed journals in the existing evidence and MOD-01, not in this board. No parallel tracking document.

### Stop rules against drift

- One implementation/qualification batch at a time. Contract questions block their own slice, not every independent feature.
- No repeated green gate or unchanged failed attempt without a specific new acceptance question, changed inputs or repaired observer. A new directory/date is not a reason.
- An unexpected failure gets one bounded assessment: failing receipt, scope affected and proposed remedy. Do not silently turn feature delivery into a compiler/debugger/harness project; ask before expanding that investigation.
- If a batch produces neither a functional result, a necessary accepted gate nor a resolved named blocker, report that immediately. Do not spend a second batch expanding the same investigation without user agreement.
- No new ledger, global cell census, framework, baseline change, public-contract widening or weaker assertion/budget merely to make this board advance.
- Existing authorization for selective validated commits does not authorize unrelated changes, accepted gaps or unvalidated runtime integration. Local synthetic acceptance does not certify vendor accounts, entitlements or service interoperability.
